### 2026-11-02 22:50（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2117267989628387531
- 原文：
  > October Performance
  > 
  > - Trades: 6 
  > - Win/rate: 50%
  > - Net Return: 12R
  > - Max Drawdown: 1.3%
  > - Performance by Hour: 5-6 AM
  > - Performance by Weekday: Wednesday & Friday
  > 
  > This data does not include my current short position from 81k that's up 4R.
  > 
  > Some data that stands out to me is the hour and days of my profitable setups (5-6AM + Wed / Frid). This is often an overlooked data point, for me these figures tell me when I need to be at the charts and mentally locked in for a trade setup. 
  > 
  > At the start of the month most of my losses came from breakout trades, that was my sign that momentum was cooling off on alts and that I should start focusing my attention back to trading BTC.
  > 
  > Initially I did not have a clear read on BTC in terms of a trade setup, but I did have a bias in term of favouring shorts as we accepted back within the weekly range high.
  > 
  > The first short trade I executed was a Range Extreme (Internal Sweep) and the second was a Mean Reversion (Internal Sweep). 
  > 
  > Main reason these setups played out nicely was because I was flowing with the trend, add on top the fact that when I did not have a clear read on the market, I stayed flat and waited instead of taking sub-par setups. 
  > 
  > Conclusion: 
  > 
  > Overall, I am very pleased with how I traded this month. Manged to switch my focus from alts back to BTC when momentum was cooling off.
  > 
  > When I did not have a clear setup on BTC, I simply waited for one to present itself instead of trying to force trades.
  > 
  > Did not make any mistakes when it came to the trade lifecycle (Execution, Management, Closure).
  > 
  > Heading into the November, my goals remain the same which is to only execute playbook trades and of course provide as much free value to everyone on X.
- 附图：https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HWHdtwyXMAE2GIx.jpg https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HWHdtw4WkAAyLey.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HWHdrEhWkAAiY76.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HWHds-dXgAAjd2j.png

  > bitcoin:native (Full Breakdown + Setups)
  > 
  > (Weekly)
  > 
  > The weekly chart is used primarily for identifying my HTF bias. We can see how price is still trading within the HTF range (84k -> 60k) which concludes price is in a rotational environment.
  > 
  > However, the weekly 12/25 EMA's have flipped bullish. This makes me believe that we are in a Re-Accumulation range and that any higher lows should provide good opportunities for swing longs. 
  > 
  > (Daily)
  > 
  > The Daily chart is more for trade location and more current market context aka (Range or Trending).
  > 
  > In this case, you will see that I've marked out a box which represent a smaller range (88k -> 75k) within the HTF one. This is something I like to do to provide more clarity with the current PA.
  > 
  > The daily bands are bearish and sitting at 81-82k, this is the level I want to see capping price in order to see more downside.
  > 
  > (4H)
  > 
  > Now let's take it a step further with the range refinement.
  > 
  > Price on the 4H is capped between 88k and 80k, we can treat this as our LTF range but you have to keep the bigger picture in mind when it comes to which trade direction (Long / short) is the most favourable.
  > 
  > With price currently at 80k, the daily + 12H stoch oversold, the R/R is just not there for me to be chasing shorts at current levels.
  > 
  > (Trade Setups)
  > 
  > I only want to focus on the Higher probability setups instead of my other ones.
  > 
  > With that in mind, the levels that offer the best R/R are going to be those key swing highs and lows from the daily chart.
  > 
  > This includes looking for shorts if price pushes up into 88k  and looking for longs if price sweeps the 75k swing lows.
  > 
  > This phase of trading (Planning Phase) is very easy, anyone can do it. It's just a matter of identifying where you want to trade and setting some alerts.
  > 
  > The execution is what separates the actual traders because they know exactly what they want to see to execute and are able to put on that risk in the moment when emotions are high.

### 2026-10-19 21:30（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2112174427328622794
- 原文：
  > How to mark out your "Ranges": 👇
  > 
  > There is a big misconception when it comes to drawing your ranges and that is that there is only 1 correct way of doing it.
  > 
  > The reality is that's not the case.
  > 
  > If you prefer trading Swing failures then marking out the range from via the wicks is better, including marking out the external highs / lows.
  > 
  > However, if you prefer deviations instead of swing failures then consider marking out your ranges more around S/R levels instead of swing points.
  > 
  > Another important factor to consider when drawing your ranges comes down to the timeframe.
  > 
  > What I mean by timeframe is what is your horizon for the trade? 
  > 
  > Are you looking for a HTF swing or simply looking to trade key S/R levels within a larger range.
  > 
  > Below I have shared 4 ways that you can mark out a range using the ETH chart, each with their own little nuances when it comes down to what setups are better suited.
  > 
  > Let me know in the comments how you would approach each of them?
- 附图：https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HU_qEDwXkAAVoDf.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HU_qLGaWIAE8eVR.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HU_qUX6WEAAz1xF.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HU_qa4lWsAAfhl-.png

  > $BTC (Full-Breakdown w/ Setups)
  > 
  > Weekly
  > 
  > My bias remains that price is in a range bound environment. 
  > 
  > Due to having the weekly EMA's crossing bullish during this range, I am leaning more towards it being a accumulation range.
  > 
  > Weekly Stochastic is also Overbought which simply means I am not interested in chasing any swing long positions at current levels.
  > 
  > Daily
  > 
  > The daily stochastic RSI is oversold while price has swept the local lows at 80k.
  > 
  > The way that I interpret this is that unless price sweeps a local high and the Daily or 12H Stoch oscillates back into the highs then I will stay flat on perp positions. 
  > 
  > Daily EMA's are capping price in the short term but would be much more interested in seeing a push above then back below followed by a bearish cross on the EMA's.
  > 
  > Setup #1: Short
  > 
  > Looking for an internal sweep of 84.3k, which is also previous weekly high.
  > 
  > The confluences I would look for on this setup are either the 12H or daily Stoch overbought + Heavy buyers' absorption above the swing point.
  > 
  > Setup #2: Long
  > 
  > This is my higher conviction swing long scenario where price sweeps the key swing low at 75k which is confluent with the Weekly 12/25 EMA's.
  > 
  > This reminds me a lot of previous cycle bottom when we made a higher low, retested a key swing low and the weekly EMA's prior to breaking out of the accumulation range.

### 2026-10-15 20:23（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2110708232180121838
- 原文：
  > Having a notebook to write things down is one of the best additions to my trading over the past few years. 
  > 
  > I typically start off every morning with writing down my thoughts on the market, regardless of how strong my read is.
  > 
  > At the end of the day I’ll then write down what happened so I can compare both.
  > 
  > When I’m uncertain or have no read on the market I’ll also write down my thoughts.
  > 
  > The goal is that when I experience a similar environment then I can look back at how I traded / reacted the previous time it happened. 
  > 
  > This is the type of stuff that’s helped me the most in understanding my thinking and patterns.

  > The majority of your gains come from a small amount of your trades. 
  > 
  > Those small amount of trades are A* setups.
  > 
  > Those A* setups are where you size up more on those trades. 
  > 
  > You size up on trades when you have the conviction to support.
  > 
  > You have conviction through gathering data on your trades. 
  > 
  > You gather data thought journaling you trades. 
  > 
  > Conclusion = Journal

  > $BTC
  > 
  > I don't have any clear read in terms of short-term PA at the moment.
  > 
  > I see an argument for both side of the coin:
  > 
  > On one hand you can argue that price retest a demand zone on 4H and is now back above the Range high and because weekly 12/25 EMA's are crossed over then the context remains bullish.
  > 
  > On the other hand, you could say price was in a HTF range since February. We broke out of the range and held for over a week, yet price failed to show any momentum to the upside. Thus treating it as a deviation.
  > 
  > Last week at took my attempt at longing a LTF compression above the range high and got stopped out on that setup.
  > 
  > The only setups that are part of my trading playbook would be a retest of the weekly EMA's (75k) confluent with the Swing low or the deeper pullback into the mind-range confluent with the single print.
  > 
  > To be clear, I'm not saying we will go there but I just know those are levels that align with my system so I will simply set alerts and wait until I get more clarity on short term PA.

### 2026-10-05 22:24（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2107114720796852469
- 原文：
  > Since the 3.5 years I’ve been trading, the majority of my career PnL has been from the last couple months.
  > 
  > Everyone wants the end result but switch to being gamblers when they realize it takes years to build a strong trading foundation. 
  > 
  > What’s a foundation? 
  > 
  > - Journaling your trades 
  > - Consistently reviewing them
  > - Understand Market Context
  > - Price action concepts 
  > - Orderflow (Bonus)
  > - Risk management 
  > - Building Playbooks (Setups + execution)
  > 
  > These are all things nobody is going to do for you.
  > 
  > If you’re lucky the same way I was then you will have people like @Trader_XO who consistently push people to focus on the process and building a foundation as opposed to constantly obsessing over the outcome of trades. 
  > 
  > I wouldn’t be where I am today without the knowledge he’s shared and I also wouldn’t be here without myself actually putting in the work.

  > After one of my best trading months, the conclusion is very simple...

  > I am still the same trader I was the month before, just because I had a very good month does not miraculously make me a better trader.

### 2026-09-28 20:16（北京时间）
- 类别：指标　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2104545901586846122
- 原文：
  > $JUP
  > 
  > "I missed the initial breakout, so the trade is over"
  > 
  > Jup is a prime example on how utilizing the left side of Price action can help you get back into trades.
  > 
  > Price broke out of the first accumulation range which is when I executed the trade.
  > 
  > You then have another clean range from 0.32$ to 0.66$ that can be traded.
  > 
  > Not to mention that in some cases, price will retest the initial breakout zone. In this instance we have a demand zone right below the breakout level which provides another opportunity to get onboard the trade.
  > 
  > It's not about trying to predict which scenario price is going to give you, it's about having a plan for the scenarios you're willing to trade and then waiting to see which one gets triggered.
- 附图：https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HTTX21sXYAAp5X6.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HTTX9QWW8AAY7Mv.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HTTYIdkXIAAtLsF.png https://pbs.twimg.com/media/HTTZCb-XsAArijZ.png

  > This will probably save you a lot of money:
  > 
  > We know that when btc breaks out of a range and starts trending, that's when alts also follow suit and pump with btc.
  > 
  > However, when btc then starts to form a new range. Alts enter of period of downtrend instead of ranging like btc.
  > 
  > You can test this out yourself by taking an alt chart and overlapping it with btc and analyze the relationship during last cycle.
  > 
  > Bonus tip is that when btc bottoms out during its re-accumulation range, most alts will do so as well. 
  > 
  > What that means for me is that if I am in swing longs on alts and btc has broken out of a range, once my system triggers that btc is going to start ranging. I will be closing out my swing longs.

### 2026-09-23 18:11（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2102702500226695537
- 原文：
  > The biggest thing I've noticed over the past few years when trading breakouts is that just because price retest your entry, it does not mean your trade is invalidated.
  > 
  > The amount of breakout's where price comes back to entry, you think it will stop you out, you close the trade.
  > 
  > Next thing you know price then rips up and your left sidelined because you could not handle taking the loss.
  > 
  > This concept is the same for both diagonal and horizontal breakouts.
  > 
  > Just look at accumulation ranges and compressions and start building a playbook of all the different variations of price action.
  > 
  > I thrive in know all the little nuances of all my trading setups.

- 原文：
  > On track to have my best performing month this year.
  > 
  > Currently sitting at 17R, and 15R+ (Unrealized).
  > 
  > Is this a representation of my performance every month? Absolutely fucking not...
  > 
  > This reflects years of complete dedication to the craft, only a few times a year to these conditions present themself and knowing when to put your foot down is what sets most apart.
  > 
  > The reality is that when you have playbooks created to capitalize on these conditions, focusing on strength as opposed to laggers. Then this is where majority of your gains will come from.
  > 
  > I'll finish off with this. RISK MANAGEMENT is the most important.
  > 
  > I could be opening a trade for every setup that presents itself but I know when I did this in the past it did more harm than good.
  > 
  > Hence why my rules are simple:
  > 
  > - I have a limit on how many positions I have open.
  > - I have a limit on how much % risk of account is risked.
  > - I size up on stronger assets and reduce on weaker ones.

  > $ETH (Full Breakdown + Setups)
  > 
  > Weekly
  > 
  > Price remains trapped within the HTF range "4,8k-1,5k".
  > 
  > Similar to btc, price is trading above the weekly 12/25 EMA's and the bands are likely to cross bullish over the next few days. 
  > 
  > Daily
  > 
  > Price is trading slightly above the current range high (2,466$) and if you look at the daily stochastic, it has fully reset into the lows.
  > 
  > In most cases that signals a momentum reset, and price is likely to make a second leg. This is simply what my data points towards.
  > 
  > 4H - Setup #1
  > 
  > There is a very clean 4H S/R level that has been capping price in terms of no candle closure above 6 times over the past few weeks.
  > 
  > I'll be looking for a breakout once we flip that level for a trade into the 2.8k -> 3k region which is a HTF supply zone confluent with the Yearly Open level.
  > 
  > Setup #2
  > 
  > Similar to BTC, I'm looking to bid any flushes into the 2,1k region. You have confluence with the mid-range, single print, Weekly 12/25 EMA's, HTF S/R level, 0.618 Fib.
  > 
  > That would be a setup I look to take back into the range high and beyond.
  > 
  > Conclusion
  > 
  > My short term bias is leaning more towards a breakout on the flip of the 4H S/R level, not going to force the trade but if it triggers then the R/R is worth it.
  > 
  > I don't know when we will get a pullback or if we will and the reality is that I don't care.
  > 
  > I have a plan for it, that's the only thing that matters to me.

  > Daily
  > 
  > Price front ran the range high (82,888$) and has been consolidating just below it. The Daily Stochastic RSI has also had a momentum reset during this period.
  > 
  > Daily Stoch resets when price is consolidating below a range high in most cases signals another leg, at least into the range high. 
  > 
  > The Daily 12/25 EMA's have also been supporting price during it's consolidation & momentum reset.
  > 
  > 4H
  > 
  > A few weeks ago we had a Three Drives Pattern, Thea reason I did not like it was because price did not sweep a key high in the process.
  > 
  > After the initial rejecting from the Three drives, you notice how price made a higher low, that is a sign of strength. 
  > 
  > I want to see higher lows continue to be respect in the short term which increases the likelihood that we trade into the range highs + more. 
  > 
  > Setup #1
  > 
  > Fast spike into the Range high with buyer absorption followed by acceptance back below the RH. 
  > 
  > What is important with this setup is watching price make a lower low after the execution. 
  > 
  > If we continue to make a higher low and fail to breakdown then be prepared for high 80s.
  > 
  > Setup #2
  > 
  > Deeper pullback scenario in case of a large flush event. Looking to bid a retest of 71-72k.
  > 
  > This level is confluent with the weekly 12/25 EMA's, Mid-range, Single Print (TPO), 0.618 fib retracement.
  > 
  > Conclusion
  > 
  > In all honestly, most of my attention is on alts as that is where I have been getting the most returns. This is why I choose to be very selective with where I want to trade on BTC.
  > 
  > What I will always keep an eye on is where all the key infliction points are on BTC as when we start trading into key levels, that's where you need to be aware of your downside risk and start to lock in gains accross your positions.

### 2026-09-05 22:31（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2096244762474926341
- 原文：
  > This is the best thing you can do this weekend: 
  > 
  > 1) Review each trade you took and find the most common mistakes.
  > 
  > Did you take a trade and as it was approaching you take profit you decided to extend your targets only to watch all your gains gone after the market flushed?
  > 
  > -> Don’t adjust your TP, you can however leave a runner. 
  > 
  > Did price trade back to your entry and you decided to close it because you didn’t want to take a stop loss? Likely due to oversizing. 
  > 
  > -> Accept the risk before you open a trade. The trade is invalided when your stop is hit, not when it’s trading against you. 
  > 
  > Did you take every setup that triggered and end up with more trades then you can successfully managed? 
  > 
  > -> Focus on the strongest setups and size up on them.

### 2026-09-01 21:44（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2094783392420086254
- 原文：
  > August Performance Review
  > 
  > Net Return: 19R
  > Win/Rate: 66%
  > Avg Return: 6.3R
  > Profit Factor: 9.5R
  > Drawdown: -1R
  > Trades: 3
  > 
  > This was my far my most profitable month this year. June being in second place with 15R (This was the BTC swing short from 80k to 60k)
  > 
  > I have been averaging 3-4R a month with both May and June being outliers at 10R+ so it's important to show that this month's performance is not a representation of my monthly average.
  > 
  > One trade in August carried majority of the PnL and that was my ETH breakout long. I was originally treating the consolidation as distribution then I changed by bias to looking for breakout.
  > 
  > The ability to change you bias when presented with new information was key.
  > 
  > Important to mention that this trade was never meant to be a huge position for me. Initially my targets were more conservative looking for 2,100$ but as price continued to show strength, I kept the position open trailing my stop. 
  > 
  > These are the kind of trades that happen for me once or twice a quarter. Therefore, I know not to treat or manage every position the same way.
  > 
  > This monthly performance does not make me a better trade than I was last month or the previous one. 
  > 
  > Heading into September, the previous month is forgotten, and the focus remains on executing trades in my playbook, following my rules, sticking to strick risk management and continuing to journal and review every trade.

### 2026-08-30 21:07（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2094049283838267560
- 原文：
  > One of the biggest mistakes I made last cycle was not focusing hard enough on strong alts. 
  > 
  > You’re better off playing LTF compression on strong coins instead of trying to front run the rotations on laggers. 
  > 
  > Same goes for if you have a watchlist of 20+ alts. 
  > 
  > The goal isn’t to take every single one of them, but instead focus on the winners and keep compounding positions on them. 
  > 
  > I’d rather size up 2-4% on a strong alt than have 4% spread across 4 different positions.

### 2026-08-17 23:57（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2089381132714279225
- 原文：
  > If I were to start trading from scratch, here’s the advice I would give myself:
  > 
  > 1) Process > Outcome
  > 
  > I’m sure like most of you, when I first started trading. My attention and focus was on the outcome (Fancy word for money). 
  > 
  > Eventually I started to become passionate about the game of trading and its complexity. 
  > 
  > Once I realized that outcome is simply a bi-product of a solid process and respecting that process then I knew where to focus my attention. 
  > 
  > 2) One size does not fit all 
  > 
  > Everyone has different tolerances, emotional strength and weaknesses. 
  > 
  > To assume that one strategy is fit for everyone or that one solution to a problem is the only one is simply a lie. 
  > 
  > At the start, you should test different styles of trading, scalping, day trading, swing, etc. To find what fits your personality the best. 
  > 
  > I am generally a risk averse person so I naturally gravitated towards swing trading and focusing on key infliction points that offer the best R/R.
  > 
  > 3) Trading Foundation 
  > 
  > A trading foundation for me consistent of having a strong understanding of market context, your strategy, price action / Orderflow concepts, and yourself.
  > 
  > @Trader_XO was one of the few people to push me to focus on building my trading foundation first before increasing my trading size. 
  > 
  > Taking a few weeks off from trading to rebuild my playbooks, build execution playbooks and complete my trading manual was the single best thing I did in 2026. 
  > 
  > 4) Risk Management
  > 
  > Surprise, surprise. XO was also one of the most vocal people when it came to having strict risk management. 
  > 
  > “You are a risk manager first, trader second”
  > 
  > When I first started trading, after a couple oversized trades. I stuck to a 1% risk rule, that was the best decision I made at it allowed me to extend my runway and continue to learn. 
  > 
  > 5) Time does not equal Quality 
  > 
  > The final piece of advice I want to give is that how much you work does not equal good work. 
  > 
  > My first year of trading, I truly cannot remember doing anything useful apart from sitting on my ass 12h a day, finding excuses to skip the gym and no socials just so I can be behind a screen “trading”.
  > 
  > Once I started to spend less time at the desk and instead focus on quality work such as building a trading playbook, reviewing my journal, building out my Evernote folder. 
  > 
  > That had a far greater impact on my trading. 
  > 
  > Bonus: Focus on yourself and not others, slow and steady wins the race.

  > Here is one of my favorite trading habits:
  > 
  > 1) Every morning after you looking at the chart, write down your observations and thoughts for how you expect the day to play out. 
  >

  > 
  > 2) At the end of the trading day, look back at your morning thoughts and compared the results 
  > 
  > The goal is not to be right every time but instead to see how in tune you are with the market and check if you missed anything important.

### 2026-08-07 17:32（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2085660355435409595
- 原文：
  > Everyone wants to be a trader until they find out what the job actually is…
  > 
  > 1.  Spend more time waiting for trades
  > 2. Journal every trade
  > 3. You’re a Risk manager 1st
  > 4. Reviewing those trades
  > 5. Weekly / Monthky / Quarterly Reviews
  > 6. Monitor edge erosion
  > 7.  Define clear trading playbooks
  > 8. Define clear execution playbooks
  > 9. Backtesting strategies 
  > 10. Forward testing those strategies
  > 11. Finding you emotional weakness 
  > 12. Finding your trading weakness
  > 13. Building solutions for each 
  > 14. Passing in setups if they don’t fit 
  > 15. Knowing when to step away from
  > 16. Not changing strats after drawdown
  > 
  > Doing all of it with nobody watching and nobody clapping.

  > Daily

  > There is also a smaller range which is the one I am primarily focusing on at the moment (67k -> 58k).

  > The main setup currently on my radar is price trading into the June High confluent with the Weekly 12/25 EMA's + Single Print, alongside the Daily Stochastic oscillating into the overbought region.
  > 
  > 4H
  > 
  > While I previously pointed out all the secondary confluences for the trade setup I'm waiting for, I wanted to also list some of the primary ones:
  > 
  > 1. Range Extreme
  > 2. Speed into Level
  > 3. Spike in Volume
  > 4. Spike in Delta
  > 5. Buyer Absorption (Shorts Closing or Longs Opening)
  > 
  > Everything else is just secondary confluence and has no impact on whether or not I take a trade.

- 原文：
  > $BTC
  > 
  > 6 times price has failed to reclaim the range low...
  > 
  > Spent some time studying price action during breakdowns of re-distribution ranges which I will share now.
  > 
  > Few things to note:
  > 
  > 1. Price respects market structure
  > 2. Lower lows -> Lower Highs
  > 3. Multiple days of overlapping value before break-away
  > 4. All the above happens below the Range low
  > 5. Final capitulation candle -> Marks start of New Range
  > 
  > Now when comparing this to the current PA, there are a few similarities and subtle differences, but the overall context is the same for me.
  > 
  > 1. Lower lows -> Lower Highs
  > 2. Multiple days of overlapping value "5-Day Comp"
  > 
  > The only subtle difference is this is all happening as price is chopping in and out of the range low.
  > 
  > If price takes out the 58k lows and puts in a Lower Low, will be looking for a lower low (Respecting Market Structure) and eventually the capitulation candle.
  > 
  > I'll consider longs if we sweep and reclaim range lows while putting in a Higher low but for now, I have not seen a reason to long.

### 2026-06-24 17:49（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2069719617166016711
- 原文：
  > How do you hold large swing trades?
  > 
  > This is a question I've been asked a few times, and I wanted to give my initial experience after holding one of my longest trades. 
  > 
  > The first step for me is making sure we are in the right market environment for a swing trade.
  > 
  > On btc we have been in a HTF downtrend which ticks the first box, the second is that ranges in a HTF downtrend are to be treated as re-distribution so understanding the nuances of re-distribution ranges was very important to me.
  > 
  > Most of these ranges have a large, failed breakout at the later stages of the range which when confirmed is when I start thinking about playing a swing trade. 
  > 
  > The next step is identifying all the key S/R levels and FTA (First Trouble Area) where price is likely to react / bounce from. 
  > 
  > If the market is truly one time framing to the downside, then you will notice a few common patterns such as:
  > 
  > 1. Price front running key swing lows.
  > 2. Price front running inefficiencies on TPO (NPOC, SP)
  > 3. Price spending lots of time failing to bounce from a swing low.
  > 4. Price keeps making Lower Lows and Lower Highs.
  > 
  > If price just smashed through key levels without any reaction, then that solidifies the argument for me to keep holding the position.
  > 
  > Another tip is something I picked up from TraderXO which is using for example the 4H 12/25 EMA's as dynamic resistance, as long as price keeps rejecting from those bands then keep holding the trade. 
  > 
  > Now for the most important part of this post....
  > 
  > In order to really hold large swing trades, you need "CONVICTION".
  > 
  > How do you get conviction? 
  > 
  > Through data.
  > 
  > How do you get data?
  > 
  > Through journaling.
  > 
  > In my 3 years of trading, the recent swing short I took was the first big swing trade I've ever taken.
  > 
  > None of this happened overnight, there were many losses and missed trades that led to this one. 
  > 
  > The reality is that I even missed the breakdown from 96k to 60k and because of that I got so mad with myself.
  > 
  > I told myself; you can never make this same mistake again and all I did was study the PA so much that next time I would see the same thing unfold I would be able to capture it as I now have a playbook for it.
  > 
  > Just don't fall into the trap of thinking every trade is a big swing trade as that's the easiest way to roundtrip and stale your account growth.
  > 
  > Hope you found some value in this post!

  > The one curse trading gives to new traders is the false expectation of profitability. 
  > 
  > You can be a complete beginner, with no system, no process, no strategy. 
  > 
  > Place a couple trades and one might be profitable. 
  > 
  > That one trade can give you enough false hope for you to start believing that maybe trading isn’t so difficult after all. 
  > 
  > The reality is unless you have a clearly defined strategy with the data to support it then you’re not trading, just gambling. 
  > 
  > Process > Outcome

  > 2. Each setup was part of my Playbook
  > 3. No emotional trades
  > 4. Capitalized on a few Altcoins rotations

  > Conclusion
  > 
  > The biggest thing I am proud of was my ability to correctly identify the market regime on BTC and position myself according.
  > 
  > There was always the chance that I would be wrong on my bias, but everything was telling me to treat the range as re-distribution.
  > 
  > For all my closed trades last month, being able to stick to my process on each of them and never deviating from my plan is a far greater accomplishment in my eyes than any monetary goal.
  > 
  > My main focus for the month of June is to continue to manage my BTC swing short from 80k and flow with the trend aka shorting any lower high bounces.
  > 
  > If you take anything from this monthly review, let it be that you do not need to take multiple trades every day.   
  > 
  > Quality > Quantity                     Process > Outcome

- 原文：
  > Well said. 
  > 
  > All it really took is for someone to take the time to study breakouts during a re-accumulation ranges to get an idea of what they “should look like”.
  > 
  > Then studying those “breakouts” in re-distribution ranges to see how those fail breakouts aka deviations differ from re-accumulation ranges on a Price action bases. 
  > 
  > Slap on your Weekly 12/25 EMAs to help define your HTF context then the rest is just a comparison game of the PA.
  > 
  > Used very little OF when execution this particular setups, only towards the end for my initiation playbook post deviation of RH.

### 2026-05-19 22:54（北京时间）
- 类别：开单/持仓　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2056750299662942692
- 原文：
  > bitcoin:native
  > 
  > "Weekly Analysis"
  > 
  > The weekly trend remains bearish as long as the weekly 12/25 EMAs are capping price. This should not be a surprise as I have been extremely vocal about treating this range as re-distribution.
  > 
  > There are also similar confluences to the 28th of March 2022 fractal where price traded above the weekly bands while the weekly stochastic RSI was overbought and the RSI was retesting the mid-level.
  > 
  > But the most important part of this comparison is the fact that in both scenarios the context is bearish. 
  > 
  > "Daily Analysis"
  > 
  > After spending 11 Days above the range high without any real follow through to the upside, we have accepted back into the range thus confirming the deviation.
  > 
  > Price is also below the Daily 12/25 EMAs, what I want to see next is the bands cross bearish and secondly is for the bands to act as resistance on the way down. Similar to the end of January 2026.
  > 
  > Daily Stochastic is oversold, most would assume price is due a bounce but then most don't understand that you must adjust how you use some indicators according to the market context.
  > 
  > We are in a HTF downtrend, and we just accepted back into the range after a failed breakout. The daily stochastic RSI is very likely to remain oversold as price continues to trade lower.
  > 
  > "4H Analysis"
  > 
  > There is a H12 supply zone around 78k which is where I will be looking for possible mean reversion setups if we trade back into that region.
  > 
  > You probably have also noticed how I have market out where the daily 12/25 EMAs are and that is because they can provide a secondary form of confluence for the trade setup.
  > 
  > At 75k is where we had the final Higher low so ideally, I want to see that level broken to confirm the shift in market structure (This just gives me more confidence to hold the short).
  > 
  > Main target for my swing short is 66k as that is where I believe we are likely to see a relief bounce. There is a level prior that I will be observing which is 70k, this is also where I will look to close the mean reversion trade.
  > 
  > If you found value from this breakdown then I would appreciate any Likes / Comments / Retweets! 🫶

### 2026-04-14 19:24（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2044013845044162907
- 原文：
  > When / How should you increase your trading size?
  > 
  > If you don't yet have any edge in trading, then your objective is not to make money but instead preserve it. 
  > 
  > You shouldn't be risking more than 0.5% on a trade, I'd even recommend less than that as your goal is to collect data to solidify an edge.
  > 
  > If you already have an established edge and have a good enough data sample of what your best performing setups are then you can start thinking about sizing dynamically into your trades.
  > 
  > Then comes the How?
  > 
  > A big mistake that I used to make in the past was increasing my size too much which ended up doing more harm than good.
  > 
  > While I don't have a very specific / robotic way of sizing up I do follow a few basic principles such as not increasing my size by more than 20% at a time.
  > 
  > Example: If you risk 100$ per trade and you want to increase your size then you increase it to 110$ (10%) or 120$ (20%).
  > 
  > You want to size up enough so that you are in discomfort, but not too much to the point where you start making more mistakes (Closing Early, failing to take trades).
  > 
  > In the end, I don't believe there is a one approach fits all. But with what I have shared above, it should provide some guidance into how you think about sizing up.

- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创

- 原文：
  > Update on the AI tool: 
  > 
  > A TLDR of what I’ve been spending most of my time on is fine tuning my AI “Trading Coach” where it’s main purpose is finding patterns in my trading / thought process before they become bad habits. 
  > 
  > At the moment I simply input my thoughts throughout the day whether it’s on the market or based on how I feel and at the end of the week I get a full report. 
  > 
  > The problem is that this doesn’t really cover the area where most mistakes are made which are during the trade lifecycle. 
  > 
  > That’s why the main change I’m going to make to the tool is have it separately track my thoughts during the Execution, Management and closure phase of the trade. 
  > 
  > Now what’s important to understand is that the quality of the results will be dependent on how honest and frequent I made the updates during the trades lifecycle. 
  > 
  > That’s it for now, will update as I find more ways to improve it!

### 2026-03-16 21:45（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2033540034981482848
- 原文：
  > My plan for implement AI as a trader: 👇
  > 
  > My main focus as of lately has been on figuring how I can improve aspects of my trading using AI.
  > 
  > Now the first thing to make clear is that for now I have no intention in trying to automate any aspects of my trading. I love manually identifying my levels and filling in my market prep sheets. 
  > 
  > There are two areas where I see the most value for me:
  > 
  > 1) Finding Patterns in my emotions / thought process.
  > 
  > I have a notebook where I write down all my market thoughts throughout the day. This allows me to look back at any day throughout the year and know exactly what I was thinking.
  > 
  > AI can be quicker at reviewing all those notes and be able to find any patterns / holes in my thought process.
  > 
  > This has been the main project I have been working on for the past few weeks and continue to refine.
  > 
  > 2) Equities Dashboard
  > 
  > A few friends and I have been building playbooks to start trading equities and one aspect that I want to improve at is being able to analyse companies from a fundamental standpoint as opposed to simply technicals.
  > 
  > This is because when it comes down to your conviction on a setup, I believe having a strong fundamental driver can help justify when to size up more on setups.
  > 
  > My plan on how to implement AI comes in two parts:
  > 
  > A) Create a dashboard for filtering my "Stocks in Play" based on the existing playbooks I have.
  > 
  > B) Have a fundamental analysis dashboard that is able to breakdown companies and sectors via based on the metrics I deem important.
  > 
  > The latter will in my opinion be the most challenging as I have very little experience with fundamentals, but I also believe it will be the most beneficial to my trading once its complete. 
  > 
  > While I am no expert by any means with AI tools and I am very overwhelmed with everything that keeps coming out and sometimes I am not sure where to start.
  > 
  > There has been one account that I highly recommend following for anyone new to all this AI shit and that is @milesdeutscher  , he's been deep in the trenches since the start and continues to provide alpha everyday.

- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/2003142900700340594
- 原文：
  > When I first started posting on X in 2024, I made a promise to myself to stay true to my values and always prioritize Quality > Quantity. 
  > 
  > I knew that doing so wasn’t the “fastest” way to grow an account and that’s never been a priority of mine.
  > 
  > Honesty is what has helped me grow. Not trying to sell people on a dream of “Trading is easy”. Trading is the most mentally challenging things I’ve ever done. 
  > 
  > I’ve given it my all everyday for the past two years, haven’t even taken a holiday since. Although I don’t recommend this, I love the game so much that the thought of not working on my system doesn’t cross my mind.
  > 
  > Sharing my thoughts / Ideas on X to you all has truly been a blessing, grateful for the new friendships. 
  > 
  > The most important lesson I have learnt over the past few years is this: “Always stay true to yourself and your values, the rest will fall into place”
  > 
  > Lots of big things planned for 2026 that I look forward to sharing with you all soon 🤫

### 2025-11-07 02:58（北京时间）
- 类别：行情判断　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/1986508464542425106
- 原文：
  > The importance of journaling your emotions 👇
  > 
  > As a trader, we all have moments where our emotional state becomes elevated and during these periods our judgement and decision making can be impaired.
  > 
  > On the 4th of November as price was trading towards the 98k swing low which we ended up frontrunning.
  > 
  > I could feel my emotions creeping in, what I decided to do was take a piece of pen and paper and write down all the emotions I was feeling as well as breaking down the price action / flows and what my expectations would be for price.
  > 
  > Over the next few days, I then would look back at what I wrote down and try to relive those thoughts and emotions.
  > 
  > I then would write down what ended up happening to compare it with the thoughts I had written down in the moment.
  > 
  > What I have seen is my emotions spike when I don't have a plan and, in this instance, even if I had a plan for the 98k sweep, I did not have a plan for price front-running the level which meant that I was trying to improvise plans in the moment while I am watching everything unfold (Not something I recommend).
  > 
  > TLDR: Journal your emotions and what triggered them so you can better understand yourself.

  > $BTC
  > 
  > These are the two levels I am looking to trade around over the coming week. 👇
  > 
  > Yesterday we front ran the 98k swing low which was the main level I wanted to execute from and still do. 
  > 
  > The question is do we squeeze up higher first before taking it out?
  > 
  > When we failed to sweep 98k we had a heavy delta cluster forming which was my first sign that we could see a local bottom as the hit rate w/ clusters is significant.
  > 
  > Scenarios are as followed: 
  > 
  > 1) Squeeze up into 109k -> 110k supply zone confluent with the monthly open (NPOC + SP) and 5-Week Comp VAL, targeting the 98k low for a double bottom or rounded retest.
  > 
  > 2) Trades back into the equal lows and while doing so take out the SFP at 98k into the demand zone at 97.7k -> 96k confluent with a SP.
  > 
  > No clue which one happens first, and it's not my job to predict.
  > 
  > I only care about having a clear plan for both scenarios and waiting to for either one to give me a trigger to execute the trade.

### 2025-11-03 02:18（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/1985048993324298663
- 原文：
  > Monthly Performance Review (October)
  > 
  > For clarity, I started a new trading account where I must abide by specific rules such as a Daily + Weekly max drawdown. My goal as mentioned in a longer post is to trade for a prop firm and no not your fake prop firms.
  > 
  > The challenge I am doing started in October (Q4), so the data only consist of the trades taken that month and is not a representation of my yearly performance as the trade sample is small (5 trades) taken this month. 
  > 
  > I plan to add the new stats on top every month to provide a better representation of my performance. Ideally you want a sample of at least 30+ trades per setup at minimum.
  > 
  > I will share my review in a written format and also with pictures, do take into consideration that this data is also not verified by anyone so could all be bs like majority of peoples pnls 🤣
  > 
  > Trade Breakdown
  > 
  > Number of trades: 5 (4 short / 1 long) (All BTC)
  > 
  > Win/rate: 60% (3 wins / 2 losses)
  > 
  > Max Account Drawdown: 0.51%
  > 
  > Return / Drawdown: 9.41
  > 
  > Biggest winner: 2.88%
  > 
  > Biggest looser: 0.51%
  > 
  > Profit Factor: 5.73
  > 
  > Winner Avg Hold Time: 2.3 (Days)
  > 
  > Best Setup: Mean reversion + SFP (Pro-Trend)
  > 
  > Worst Setup: Mean reversion (Counter trend trade)
  > 
  > Average Bet Size: 0.5%
  > 
  > Performance by day: Tuesday (3 wins) & Thursday (2 losses)
  > 
  > Net Return: 4.76% (Still have some short unrealized)
  > 
  > P.S
  > 
  > Unfortunately, in order to share what my Sortino, Sharpe and Calmar ratio would accurately be I need a minimum sample size of 10 trades (Based on Edgewonk). Hopefully next month or in December I can share those.
  > 
  > Conclusion of Trade Review
  > 
  > This will be a little summary on the mistakes that I made this month across all my trades as well as what I plan to do moving forward as the new month starts to help mitigate making these mistakes again.
  > 
  > 1) Did not have Limit TP order set when in the trade which led to me roundtripping a few "R".
  > 
  > - Bi-product of this was that it was harder for me to mentally reset which effects my ability to spot / take other setups.
  > 
  > Conclusion: TP limit orders will be set when execution the trade.
  > 
  > 2) Need to build execution playbook around "Three Drives Pattern". While the trade worked out for me great when I shorted 123k, the lack of an execution playbook meant that my R/R on the trade was not as good as it could have been compared to if I had a clear playbook around execution the three drives pattern
  > 
  > Conclusion: Develop execution playbook for "Three Drives".
  > 
  > 3) One of my losses was the long trade which I was simply too early to taking the trade. I did not follow my rules of waiting for LTF range to form and seeing compression of bands on 15M which is my filter. 
  > 
  > Conclusion: I will have a sperate chart open on the 15M timeframe with the 12/25 EMA's on them at all times that way it is visible to me throughout the day.

### 2025-10-24 05:00（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/1981465668685091293
- 原文：
  > Little story on my 2.5 year trading journey: 
  > 
  > My first year of trading is what I would call a wasted year, this is because I was pretty much a headless chicken. 
  > 
  > No plan, no structure, no process. Just ambition to make quick easy money which after my first month I realized was not going to be the case at all.
  > 
  > The best thing I had going for myself was how strict I was with my risk management. 
  > 
  > 1) Because I didn’t like to loose money 🤣
  > 
  > 2) SZ would always preach using a fixed 1% risk if your a new trader which I was.
  > 
  > After my first year of trading was over, this is where things started to become much more structured. All credit to XO for that as majority of my knowledge came from two people and he was one of them. 
  > 
  > I remember back the he had a YouTube Live stream library of over 300+ videos which I sat and watched all of them one by one while taking notes on pen and paper at the time.
  > 
  > While I had no mentor back then, that didn’t stop me from trying to reverse engineer as much as I could from his streams. 
  > 
  > 1.5 years in, I stumbled across a humble lad called O&F, he was the second most impactful teacher along my journey. 
  > 
  > It was thanks to him that I heard for the first time the words “Playbook, System, Process and Delta 😉”
  > 
  > Not only that he also was fully transparent in sharing his own system which led to me building my first iteration of my own system. 
  > 
  > That’s when I started to really focus on taking only my playbook trades and saw my pnl curve start moving upwards after being a breakeven trader for quite some time. 
  > 
  > Heading into 2025 the goal was simple:
  > 
  > 1) Clearly define my Playbook setups
  > 2) Develop execution playbooks
  > 3) Implement Orderflow into my trading
  > 4) Journal every single trade + emotional state during the trade lifecycle.
  > 
  > In regards to Orderflow, I made sure to study and backtest for over 6 month before implementing any of it into my existing playbooks. 
  > 
  > As I now approach 2.5 years into my career the main thing that’s been on the back of my head is where do I see myself in the future? 
  > 
  > As someone who has always had a competitive nature, I want to continuously push myself to see what kind of trader I can truly become. 
  > 
  > What I realized was that unfortunately CT wasn’t going to be the place where that was going to happen. 
  > 
  > Once I get introduced to the world of “Proprietary Trading Firms” I realized that this was my next calling. 
  > 
  > If you want to be the best then being in an environment surrounded by some of the best traders in the world is where I need to be. 
  > 
  > Q3-Q4 2025 has all been about refining my playbooks to eventually apply to a prop firm for 2026
  > 
  > This will be a part of my journey that I look forward to sharing with you all soon.

- 类别：指标　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/1977388797953266006
- 原文：
  > $BTC
  > 
  > Looking back at two instances where we have large liquidations you can see how the recovery / trajectory had upwards momentum. 
  > 
  > Both examples (5th December + 5th March 2024) also came back to retest the origin of the sell-off.
  > 
  > What's interesting with the current Liquidation is how the trajectory of Price is much more rounded / in a subtle downwards trajectory.
  > 
  > Could this be due to the intensity of the Liquidation far exceeding the previous examples? Yes, possible.
  > 
  > But it's important to note that the two previous examples happened when price was in a clear daily up-trend after already breaking out of previous re-accumulation ranges.
  > 
  > This time price was clearly range-bound during this Liquidation event which could also explain the not so speedy recovery alongside the size of the liq.
  > 
  > Obviously, with Taco man in power it won't take much for new to come out and see a strong V-recovery back into origin of the sell-off. 
  > 
  > I am leaning more to price trading lower and filling in part of the wick before any mean reversion to the upside based on:
  > 
  > 1) The liquidation happening in a range bound environment.
  > 
  > 2) The intensity of the liquidation relative to previous ones.

### 2025-10-09 21:23（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/1976277390545269178
- 原文：
  > How I will build a new system from scratch:
  > 
  > I have never traded stocks before but the desire to expand my playbook across different markets has now given me the itch.
  > 
  > For anyone who might also be in my shoes I want to share how I plan to approach developing a system.
  > 
  > 1) Lance Breitstein has made 2 live streams going over "stocks in play", I plan to watch both and start populating a folder in Trading View with all the tickers.
  > 
  > 2) Once I have all the tickers, I am then going to want to focus on period of time where stocks have shifted from a Ranging environment to a trending one.
  > 
  > 3) Next my focus will be on identifying the types of patterns pre-breakout as well as the possible confluences using a variety of indicators, I am already familiar / build system around.
  > 
  > 4) After identifying the common patterns, I will then break those down and start building execution playbooks for them.
  > 
  > 5) I plan on gathering at least 50+ examples per setup / playbook in order to familiarize myself more with pattern recognition.
  > 
  > 6) This will be a trend system so having a something in place for identifying when a trend is likely over will be a big part of my system. This will help me close / start to scale out of my positions.
  > 
  > Hence the importance of gathering as much data as possible based on previous occurrences. Although history doesn't repeat itself it often rhythms.

  > The goal isn’t to become a robot and remove your emotions in trading.
  > 
  > It’s about:
  > 
  > 1) Understanding what triggers the emotions.
  > 
  > 2) What stage of the trade lifecycle are they more apparent.
  > 
  > 3) What processes can you implement to better work with them. 
  > 
  > I know I will always have emotions in trading, but I don’t want my emotions preventing me from following my plan.

- 原文：
  > How I approach my trades: 👇
  > 
  > 1. What’s the market context 
  > 2. What setup playbook will I deploy 
  > 3. What levels do I want to trade 
  > 4. What do I want to see unfold at the level
  > 5. What execution playbook will I deploy
  > 5a. Where will my invalidation be (S/L)
  > 5b. Where is my FTA

- 类别：方法/教学　类型：引用

- 原文：
  > “Goal setting”
  > 
  > Monthly Review:
  > 
  > 1) Common trading mistakes throughout the month?
  > 
  > 2) What was the root of those mistakes?
  > 
  > 3) How can I adapt my process?
  > 
  > Weekly Review:
  > 
  > The purpose of my weekly review is to actively track how my new processes are handling my previous common mistakes. 
  > 
  > If I see that I still make those mistakes during the first few weeks then I need to get back to the drawing board.

### 2025-09-09 21:06（北京时间）
- 类别：方法/教学　类型：原创
- 链接：https://x.com/TraderMorin/status/1965401333290189078
- 原文：
  > 80% of my days are spent analysing data from my trading strategies.
  > 
  > The Objective: Know each of my setups inside out.
  > 
  > - What's the common execution pattern? +Variances
  > 
  > - Shorts vs Longs
  > 
  > -  MAE
  > 
  > - MFE
  > 
  > - Avg Hold time
  > 
  > - If we have outsized moves then what lead to them?
  > 
  > This is only surface level of the type of data I am gathering on each strategy. 
  > 
  > I go much more in depth with other metrics but there are some things best kept in the shadows.😉
  > 
  > If you are back testing your strategies which are still in the development stages, then nothing stops you from doing this with the data from your back test. 
  > 
  > The goal is then to adjust the back tested data with your real time data from forward testing.

  > Edge Degradation 
  > 
  > When I was first told that you never know how long your system will truly have edge in these markets it initially made me very concerned.
  > 
  > "You're saying that I'm putting in all this work and my system can just stop working in a year or two?"
  > 
  > As I progress in my journey this doesn't mentally affect anymore. That is mainly due to two reasons:
  > 
  > 1) As part of my journaling and reviewing I keep track of my edge degradation.
  > 
  > 2) There are so many ways that I can continue to refine my edge, every day I work on it and come up with new ways.
